Critically, `LockedRestoreStaging.stageEncryptedArchiveOnce` repeats it **inside** the cross-process `VaultLifecycleLock` after Android SAF file reading, eliminating the gap where enrollment could create an encrypted vault after a prior UI preflight yet before restore staging. `ApprovedNewVaultRestore.restoreFromStagedNewVault` and `EncryptedVaultStore.createFreshUnderLock` recheck the same predicate inside their write lock (allowing an already staged regular archive only for restore). `RestoreArtifactGuard` now detects dangling `.new/.bak` symlink evidence. Existing encrypted live-database opening and restore-candidate creation also refuse links/sidecars using NOFOLLOW checks.

**P03 SQLCipher key custody:** `KeystoreWrappedVaultKey` now detects base, `.new`, and `.bak` envelopes via NOFOLLOW instead of `File.exists()`. `unwrapExisting` no longer invokes Android `AtomicFile.openRead()` (which may reconcile/delete interrupted AtomicFile evidence); it reads a bounded 68-byte regular key envelope with `NOFOLLOW_LINKS`, checks file identity/mtime/length before and after, rejects simultaneous atomic sidecars and preserves the original ciphertext for explicit owner review. Existing wrapping algorithm, Keystore alias and AES-GCM layout are unchanged; no reset or key rotation is attempted. This change may still require Android/Keystore compatibility fixes after authorized compilation/runtime testing.

**Authored but UNEXECUTED Android test source:** `UnusedVaultTargetGuardInstrumentedTest` covers clean new-device vs staged states, dangling database/WAL/journal/SHM/key/legacy symlinks, staging after a new vault appeared between preflight and commit, staged `.new/.bak` link preservation, fresh enrollment rejection before key creation, wrapped-key sidecar preservation, key symlink rejection, and linked existing/candidate SQLCipher file denial. These are synthetic filesystem/security test inputs only, not original customer backups or real restored financial data.
